- Confidence: high
### F-OM-050 — Congregations on the beatification miracles, 1922–1923
- Claim: Antepreparatory 7 March (1922) and preparatory 25 July (1922) (Taylor); general congregation before Pius XI on Tuesday 30 Jan 1923 (AAS 15 p. 92 Diarium; decree p. 169; bull AAS 17 p. 341).
- Source: Taylor 1926/30 PDF 277; AAS 15 pp. 92, 169
- Source type: official | Rights: public-domain
- New to English readers: yes
- Confidence: high for 30 Jan 1923; medium for the 1922 dates (Taylor gives no year; the sequence implies 1922).
### F-OM-051 — Decree approving two miracles, 11 February 1923
- Claim: Pius XI announced the decree on Quinquagesima Sunday, 11 Feb 1923, chosen because it was the feast of the Apparition of the Immaculate Virgin near Lourdes and the eve of the anniversary of his coronation. He declared proved "the instantaneous and perfect healing of Sister Louise of Saint-Germain, of the Congregation of the Daughters of the Cross, from a most grave ulcer of the stomach, of haemorrhagic form" and "the instantaneous and perfect healing of the cleric Charles Anne from florid, haemoptoic tuberculosis in the cavity stage".
- Source: AAS 15 pp. 168–170
- Source type: official | Rights: public-domain
- Original words: « a gravissimo ulcere ventriculi, formae hemorragicae »; « a tubercolosi florida, hemoptoica, in periodo cavitaria »
- New to English readers: yes (the decree's exact diagnoses)
- Confidence: high
- Notes: The decree gives Charles Anne's first name ("Caroli"), settling the plan's question.
### F-OM-052 — Pius XI, 11 Feb 1923: "our beloved guiding-star … miracle of virtues and prodigy of miracles"
- Claim: In his allocution on the miracles decree Pius XI said, in Taylor's English: "the voice of God and the voice of His people have joined in extolling" her; he quoted Dante ("She has come down from Heaven to earth that we may know what a miracle means"); he called her "a most exquisite miniature of spiritual perfection"; he quoted Manzoni's image of the "fragile blossom" beside the pine and the fir; he said the "little way" is "not merely a possible, but an easy way for every soul"; and he ended by giving thanks "for the promises that have come to us from our beloved guiding-star, Thérèse of the Child Jesus, miracle of virtues and prodigy of miracles", and commended to her the Carmelite missionaries and the whole Church.
- Source: Taylor 1926/30 PDF 295–299 (book pp. 267–271)
- Source type: official (English) | Rights: public-domain
- Other sources: Laveille PDF 427 and note: "exquisitely fine miniature of perfect holiness"; "his dear star"; "a miracle of virtues and a prodigy of miracles". Taylor 1926/30 PDF 23 and 419 repeat "miracle of virtues and prodigy of miracles".
- New to English readers: yes
- Confidence: high that these words were reported; the Italian original was not found (not in AAS 15).
### F-OM-053 — Decree *de tuto*, 19 March 1923: "not yet a full decade"
- Claim: The general congregation of 6 Mar 1923 voted unanimously that the beatification could safely proceed; Pius XI announced it on 19 Mar 1923, feast of St Joseph. The decree opens: in "a very short course of years, which has not yet equalled a full decade", this most desired cause has completed its whole road.
- Source: AAS 15 pp. 228–231; Diarium p. 188
- Source type: official | Rights: public-domain
- Original words: « Brevi admodum annorum cursu, qui nempe nondum plenum adaequavit decennium »
- Other sources: Laveille PDF 427: in his discourse that day Pius XI called her "the true flower of love come from heaven to earth to astonish heaven and earth".
- New to English readers: yes
- Confidence: high
### F-OM-054 — The mayor of Lisieux resisted the exhumation
- Claim: The Carmel says the mayor of Lisieux refused (January 1922), then demanded 6 million francs, and finally approved on 8 Mar 1923. Taylor says the mayor "demanded, happily in vain, the sum of six million francs before he would allow her remains to be exhumed".
- Source: Carmel "Retour de Thérèse au Carmel"; Taylor 1926/30 PDF 420
- Source type: official (Carmel); old-collection | Rights: unknown; public-domain
- New to English readers: yes

### F-OM-055 — Translation of the remains to the Carmel, Monday 26 March 1923
- Claim: On Monday of Holy Week, 26 Mar 1923, the coffin was taken from the cemetery to the Carmel. About 50,000 pilgrims came; more than 200 priests (Laveille, Taylor) or nearly 300 (Carmel), the communities of the town, 20 Catholic delegations, officers, and a guard of honour from the American Legion with drawn swords (Laveille) escorted it. The Carmel says a smell of fresh roses came from the tomb at about 11 am for about 45 minutes; the coffin went on a new white hearse drawn by four white horses, covered with cloth of gold, and passed Saint-Jacques, the cathedral and the Benedictine abbey, reaching the chapel at 4 pm. The procession was over two kilometres long and silent except for rosaries and psalms, because no public cult was allowed before beatification (Laveille).
- When / where: 26 Mar 1923, Lisieux
- People: Bishop Lemonnier; Fr Rodrigo, postulator and papal delegate
- Source: Laveille PDF 428–430; Carmel "Retour de Thérèse au Carmel"; Taylor 1926/30 PDF 278, 318
- Source type: early-life; official | Rights: public-domain; unknown
- Conflict: Taylor's narrative (PDF 278) says "On the Feast of the Annunciation, March 25". His own table (PDF 318), Laveille and the Carmel say 26 March. In 1923, 25 March was Palm Sunday, so 26 March (Monday) fits "Monday of Holy Week". The evidence is clear: 26 March.
- New to English readers: partial (baseline has only the date in the plan)
- Confidence: high
- Notes: Cures reported that day: M-OM-054 to M-OM-057.
### F-OM-057 — The brief *Quod Ioannes*, 29 April 1923: what the beatification allowed
- Claim: The brief lets her be called Blessed; her body and relics may be exposed for public veneration but not carried in solemn processions; the Office and Mass may be said from the Common of Virgins with proper prayers, but only in the diocese of Bayeux and Lisieux and in all churches of the Discalced Carmelites; solemn celebrations may be held there within a year after the Vatican ceremony.
- Source: AAS 15 pp. 202–207 (dated 29 Apr 1923, signed Card. Gasparri)
- Source type: official | Rights: public-domain
- Original words: « eiusque corpus et lypsana sive relliquiae, non tamen in sollemnibus supplicationibus deferendae, publicae fidelium venerationi proponantur »
- New to English readers: yes
- Confidence: high
- Notes: The brief opens with the vision of John (Rev 14) of virgins following the Lamb.
### F-OM-058 — The beatification in St Peter's, 29 April 1923
- Claim: Laveille says 45 archbishops and bishops, all ambassadors to the Holy See (the French one specially delegated), and a huge crowd were present; after the brief was read, Bishop Lemonnier intoned the *Te Deum* and all the lights came on at once around her picture in Bernini's "Gloria", which showed her rising on clouds with two angels holding scrolls, "Way of Childhood" and "I will spend my heaven in doing good upon earth"; the Bishop of Bayeux sang the Mass. In the evening Pius XI came, got down from the *sedia*, and knelt in silent prayer; it was his first beatification. Taylor says the *Te Deum*, "reserved solely for a Canonisation", was intoned "by a happy mistake", and that Fr Anne and the old nun who nursed him were present. Taylor 1924 says France sent a thousand pilgrims and Italy some fifty thousand more.
- Source: Laveille PDF 430–432; Taylor 1926/30 PDF 276, 278; Taylor 1924 PDF 21–22
- Source type: early-life; eyewitness report | Rights: public-domain
- New to English readers: yes
- Confidence: high
### F-OM-060 — Pius XI's letter to Cardinal Vico, 14 May 1923: "a rain of roses"
- Claim: Pius XI wrote that it was God's special plan that, "not yet ten years" after the cause began and "barely in the twenty-fifth year" after her death, everything was finished; that the young girl unknown in the Carmel had become famous among all peoples for her simplicity, her angelic life, her virtues and miracles, and her "spiritual childhood", of which "she showed herself the pure teacher" in the book she wrote under obedience, "charming, with a charm not sought by art"; that "as she herself had foretold, she began and continues to send down a rain of roses from heaven upon men". He approved Vico's journey to preside over the triduum at Lisieux and told him to urge the people of Lisieux, who "happily possess the body of the blessed Carmelite within their walls", to pray at her tomb for the pope.
- Source: AAS 15 pp. 283–284 (*Pro tuo ipse officio*)
- Source type: official | Rights: public-domain
- Original words: « pergatque pluviam rosarum e caelo in homines demittere »
- New to English readers: yes
- Confidence: high
### F-OM-061 — Celebrations at Lisieux, May and August 1923
- Claim: Laveille says the Carmel chapel was enlarged; a triduum was held 28–30 May 1923 with Cardinal Vico as papal legate, the Primate of Normandy and the Bishops of Troyes and Évreux; the reliquary from Brazil was carried through poor lanes and main streets. On 6–8 Aug 1923 Cardinals Bourne, Dougherty and Touchet, with 15 bishops and mitred abbots, presided; each church had its own Pontifical Office; the reliquary stopped at her old Benedictine school, where the nuns sang the Magnificat; on 8 Aug Bourne sang Mass in the cathedral and Touchet preached to 6,000; about 100,000 were in the procession.

Taylor may mean the August feast; see gaps file.

- Confidence: high for Laveille's dates
### F-OM-062 — Cardinal Touchet credited her with saving Joan of Arc's cause
- Claim: Laveille says Cardinal Touchet, Bishop of Orléans, came to pay a debt: when the process for Joan of Arc's canonization was about to be interrupted, Thérèse, at his prayer, "caused Pius X to revoke his decision". In his panegyric (8 Aug 1923) he spoke of her role among soldiers and ended by asking the visiting cardinals to tell their people France wanted peace.
- Source: Laveille PDF 436
- Source type: early-life | Rights: public-domain
- New to English readers: yes
- Confidence: low–medium — a claim of intercession, reported by Laveille.
### F-OM-063 — Patroness of the Society of St Peter the Apostle while still only Blessed
- Claim: Taylor 1924 says she had "already … been officially chosen as the patroness of the Society of St. Peter the Apostle for the education of native priests", an honour "unusual in the case of a Beata". Taylor 1926/30 says Rome made her "protectress of the Society for multiplying native priests", dear to Pius XI, "who has just consecrated six Chinese bishops" (Oct 1926).
- Source: Taylor 1924 PDF 29 note; Taylor 1926/30 PDF 452
- Source type: old-collection | Rights: public-domain
- New to English readers: yes
- Confidence: medium — no act cited.

## Part E. Canonization (1923–1925)
### F-OM-064 — The cause of canonization was resumed in July 1923 (two dates)
- Claim: (a) The AAS Diarium says an ordinary congregation on "Tuesday 23 July 1923" discussed the "resumption of the cause of canonization of Blessed Teresa of the Child Jesus". (b) The bull says Pius XI ordered the Congregation to take up her cause on 27 Jul 1923.
- Source: AAS 15 p. 478; AAS 17 p. 342
- Source type: official | Rights: public-domain
- Notes: 23 Jul 1923 was a Monday, not a Tuesday; the Diarium date or weekday has an error. The bull may give the date of the papal rescript after the meeting. See gaps file.
- New to English readers: yes
- Confidence: medium
### F-OM-065 — Two new apostolic processes, at Parma and Mechelen, validated 11 June 1924
- Claim: The bull says the Congregation declared valid the apostolic processes built in the dioceses of Parma and Mechelen (Malines) on miracles after her beatification, and the pope confirmed this on 11 Jun 1924.
- Source: AAS 17 p. 342
- Source type: official | Rights: public-domain
- Original words: « in dioecesibus Parmensi et Mechliniensi »
- New to English readers: yes
- Confidence: high
### F-OM-066 — Congregations on the canonization miracles, 1924–1925
- Claim: Antepreparatory 12 Aug 1924 (Taylor); preparatory Tuesday 27 Jan 1925 (AAS 17 p. 86 Diarium; Taylor); general congregation before Pius XI on Tuesday 17 Mar 1925 (miracles decree AAS 17 p. 149; Diarium p. 162; Taylor).
- Source: Taylor 1926/30 PDF 280; AAS 17 pp. 86, 149, 162
- Source type: official | Rights: public-domain
- Conflict: the bull (AAS 17 p. 344, page image checked) says the general congregation was held "die septima mensis martii" (7 March). The decree and the Diarium, both earlier and closer, say 17 March. The evidence favours 17 March; the bull probably dropped "decima".
- New to English readers: yes
- Confidence: high for 17 March
### F-OM-067 — Decree approving the canonization miracles, 19 March 1925
- Claim: On 19 Mar 1925, feast of St Joseph, Pius XI declared proved: the instantaneous and perfect healing of Sister Gabriella Trimusi "from chronic arthrosynovitis of the left knee and at the same time from chronic spondylitis of the lower dorsal spine, both of tubercular nature"; and the instantaneous and perfect healing of Maria Pellemans "from chronic tuberculosis of the lungs and intestines".
- Source: AAS 17 pp. 148–150 (page 150 image checked)
- Source type: official | Rights: public-domain
- Original words: « a chronica arthrosinovite sinistri genu, simulque a chronica spondilite infimae spinae dorsualis, utraque naturae tubercularis »; « a chronica pulmonum atque intestinorum tuberculosi »
- New to English readers: yes
- Confidence: high
### F-OM-069 — Decree *de tuto* for canonization, 29 March 1925: no cause in the archives had gone so fast
- Claim: The general congregation of 24 Mar 1925 voted unanimously; Pius XI announced the decree on Passion Sunday, 29 Mar 1925. The decree opens: searching the archive and records of the Congregation, no other cause is found that reached canonization "with so swift and happy a course" since the procedure became so hedged with safeguards; "barely eleven years" had passed since the cause came before the Congregation; beatification had come two years before.
- Source: AAS 17 pp. 200–201; Diarium p. 162
- Source type: official | Rights: public-domain
- Original words: « Consulenti tabularium sacrae huius Congregationis eiusdemque fastos perlustranti alia non occurrit Causa, quae tam celeri tamque prospero actorum cursu supremam conscenderit Canonizationis metam »
- New to English readers: yes
- Confidence: high
- Notes: What set her apart (official statement of speed).
### F-OM-070 — The three consistories, March–April 1925
- Claim: Secret consistory 30 Mar 1925: Cardinal Vico summarized the lives, virtues and miracles of six beati, including her, and each cardinal gave his vote. Public consistory Thursday 2 Apr 1925: consistorial advocates pleaded the causes; the bull says advocate Giovanni Guasco spoke on her. Semipublic consistory Wednesday 22 Apr 1925 in the great hall above the Vatican palace: the bishops present voted, and Pius XI fixed 17 May for her canonization (Canisius 21 May; Postel and Barat 24 May; Eudes and Vianney 31 May, Pentecost).
- Source: AAS 17 pp. 125–128, 169–170; bull pp. 344–345
- Source type: official | Rights: public-domain
- New to English readers: yes
- Confidence: high
### F-OM-071 — The canonization formula of 17 May 1925 set her memorial on 30 September
- Claim: After the triple petition by consistorial advocate Virginio Jacoucci and replies by Nicola Sebastiani, Secretary of Briefs to Princes, Pius XI, seated, declared her a saint and ordered that "the universal Church" keep her memory "each year on her birthday [into heaven], namely 30 September, among the holy Virgins not Martyrs".
- Source: AAS 17 pp. 209–211
- Source type: official | Rights: public-domain
- Original words: « statuentes ab Ecclesia universali eius memoriam quolibet anno die eius natali, nempe xxx septembris … recoli debere »
- Conflict: F-OM-079 (the bull sets 3 October). Explained by F-OM-088.
- New to English readers: yes
- Confidence: high
### F-OM-078 — The next day: "deluge of glory"; an hour with Cardinal Dougherty on his "guiding star"
- Claim: At the audience for French pilgrims the next afternoon (18 May 1925), led by Bishop Lemonnier, Pius XI spoke in French, congratulated Lisieux on the "deluge of glory" that had come on it, spoke of his "dear little Saint" and urged them to her "little way". Taylor says that during that week, at an audience with Cardinal Dougherty, the pope talked "for over an hour of his 'guiding star'" and of the hopes he placed in her whom "you in America call the 'Little Flower of Jesus'". A group of Carmel friends, some relatives of the saint by marriage, and extern sisters from Lisieux and Caen had a special reception.
- Source: Taylor 1926/30 PDF 25
- Source type: eyewitness | Rights: public-domain
- New to English readers: yes
- Confidence: medium–high (Taylor present at the audience; Dougherty story second-hand)
### F-OM-079 — The bull *Vehementer exsultamus hodie* (17 May 1925)
- Claim: The bull, signed by Pius XI and the cardinals, tells her life, the process and the four miracles, and says: soon after her death her fame spread "because of the innumerable prodigies" by which "she seemed, as she had promised when dying, to send down a shower of roses on men"; for this reason the Church decided to decree her the highest honours "before the appointed and usual times". It orders her memory kept yearly on 3 October and noted in the Roman Martyrology. It records that Pius XI had already declared her "Patroness of the Missions of the same Order and of Novitiates". It ends: "Let us all invoke … the patronage of St Teresa of the Child Jesus, that through her intercession a rain of roses, that is, of the graces we need, may come down on us too."
- Source: AAS 17 pp. 337–347
- Source type: official | Rights: public-domain
- Original words: « Hinc factum est ut ei summos caelitum honores ante statuta et sueta tempora Ecclesia decernere statuerit »; « Memoriam autem eiusdem Sanctae … mandavimus quotannis die tertia octobris recolendam et in Martyrologio Romano notandam »
- New to English readers: yes
- Confidence: high
- Notes: Plan §9 lists a French translation on the Carmel site (not read here).
### F-OM-080 — Lisieux, July 1925: novena, chapel consecrated
- Claim: Laveille says Bishop Lemonnier held a novena of solemnities from 4 to 12 Jul 1925, beginning with the consecration of the renovated and enlarged Carmel chapel, with Pontifical Mass each morning and sermons by Père Martin; on Sunday 12 July the relics went in procession to Les Buissonnets, the Benedictines and the cathedral.
- Source: Laveille PDF 443
- Source type: early-life | Rights: public-domain
- Other sources: Taylor 1926/30 PDF 25: "In the month of July the Carmel held its triduum of thanksgiving". The Sanctuary site says the chapel was built in the 1840s and enlarged in 1923.
- New to English readers: yes
- Confidence: high
### F-OM-081 — 30 September 1925: the golden rose placed in the hand of the recumbent figure
- Claim: Cardinal Vico, papal legate for the second time, placed in the hand of the marble recumbent figure a golden rose blessed by Pius XI. After a double triduum, with preachers Père Martin, Abbé Thellier de Poncheville, Canon Barette of Brussels (who spoke on Mlle Pellemans's cure) and Archbishop du Bois de la Villerabel of Rouen, the relics were carried through the streets with more than 30 bishops, an Oriental patriarch, three cardinals and 40 national flags (Laveille). Taylor, present, says over forty mitred abbots, bishops and cardinals stood before the shrine; the procession went to the old episcopal gardens; the crowd sang Dumont's *Credo*; roses were blessed; an aviator circling overhead showered roses on the crowd; the town was illuminated, and St Pierre and St Jacques were "outlined in fire" for the first time.
- Source: Laveille PDF 444–446; Taylor 1926/30 PDF 26–27
- Source type: early-life; eyewitness | Rights: public-domain
- New to English readers: yes
- Confidence: high
### F-OM-082 — The Carmel chapel in 1925: twin altars, windows of "roses"
- Claim: Taylor describes the new chapel: an imperial diadem of roses over a baldachin outside; roses on the grille and flags of many nations inside; on the right, twin altars of the saint, dedicated to her as patroness of missionaries and as friend of France and all English-speaking nations (paid for by profits of Taylor's books); the shrine with the recumbent figure and the Brazilian casket; the "miraculous Madonna" above; stained glass showing "roses" that fell on missionaries, soldiers and the poor, one window showing the conversion in Edinburgh of Rev. Alexander Grant.
- Source: Taylor 1926/30 PDF 26; Taylor 1924 PDF 10, 28 note
- Source type: eyewitness | Rights: public-domain
- New to English readers: yes
- Confidence: high
### F-OM-083 — Pius XI to the Carmelite Order, 15 Nov 1925
- Claim: In a letter for the Order's anniversary Pius XI wished that the Carmel might "transfer its whitest flowers from its garden to heaven", like Mary Magdalene de' Pazzi and "that Teresa of the Child Jesus, to whom we so gladly, and with such joy of the Catholic world, decreed and gave the honours of the saints in this holy year".
- Source: AAS 17 p. 579
- Source type: official | Rights: public-domain
- New to English readers: yes
- Confidence: high
### F-OM-084 — Pius XI to a missionary bishop, July 1925: "have recourse also to the 'Little Flower'"
- Claim: Taylor reports that in July 1925 Pius XI told Mgr O'Gorman, Vicar Apostolic of Sierra Leone, putting his hand on his arm: "Invoke the Sacred Heart of Jesus, and the Blessed Virgin Mary, but … have recourse also to the 'Little Flower.' I wish I could tell you of the many very remarkable favours … of which I have been the object through her intercession!"
- Source: Taylor 1926/30 PDF 454–455
- Source type: popular (reported speech) | Rights: public-domain
- New to English readers: yes
- Confidence: medium
### F-OM-086 — Spread of *Histoire d'une âme* and of pictures and relics, 1898–1925
- Claim: Laveille says that from 1898 to 1925, 410,000 copies of the complete edition and two million copies of the abridged Life were sold; there were 35 translations; and 30,388,000 pictures and 17,507,000 relic-sachets and relic-pictures were distributed. Taylor (1927) says over a million copies of the French edition were sold; Dziewicki's English version appeared in 1901; Taylor's abridgement *As Little Children* (Aug 1911) reached its 175th thousand by March 1927; his complete *Sœur Thérèse of Lisieux* (Christmas 1912) had a first printing of 9,000 and reached 43,000 (45,000 by 1924); nearly two million leaflets were printed at the Orphans' Press, Rochdale.
- Source: Laveille PDF 415 and note; Taylor 1926/30 PDF 39–40; Taylor 1924 PDF 10
- Source type: early-life; old-collection | Rights: public-domain
- Other sources: *Divini amoris scientia* §3: translated with her other writings into "about 50 languages" (1997).
- New to English readers: yes
- Confidence: medium — publishers' figures.

## Part F. Patronages, liturgy, basilica, later popes (1927–2023)
### F-OM-087 — Patroness of all missions, equal with St Francis Xavier, 14 Dec 1927
- Claim: A decree of the Congregation of Rites says that devotion shows how the faithful welcomed her canonization, and that "even in the distant regions of the unbelievers" she did not fail to send her promised rain of roses; therefore "innumerable" bishops asked, and Pius XI declared her "special Patroness of all missionaries, men and women, and of the Missions existing in the whole world, equally principal with St Francis Xavier, with all the liturgical rights and privileges belonging to this title".
- Source: AAS 20 (1928) pp. 147–148 (decree dated 14 Dec 1927; signed Card. Vico, Angelo Mariani, secretary)
- Source type: official | Rights: public-domain
- Original words: « peculiarem Missionariorum omnium, sive virorum sive mulierum, necnon Missionum in toto orbe exsistentium Patronam aeque principalem cum S. Francisco Xaverio »
- New to English readers: partial (baseline has the fact)
- Confidence: high
### F-OM-089 — The Office and Mass of her feast, extended to the whole Church, 14 March 1928
- Claim: AAS 20 prints her Office (Double rank, 3 Oct): the collect « Domine, qui dixisti: Nisi efficiamini sicut parvuli, non intrabitis in regnum caelorum: da nobis, quaesumus, ita sanctae Teresiae Virginis in humilitate et simplicitate cordis vestigia sectari, ut praemia consequamur aeterna »; lessons IV–VI on her life; third-nocturn homily of St Leo the Great ("Christ loves childhood"); and the Mass: Introit "Veni de Libano, sponsa mea" (Song 4:8–9) with Ps 112 "Laudate, pueri, Dominum"; Epistle Isaiah 66:12–14; Gradual Matt 11:25; Alleluia verse Sirach 39:17–19 ("like a rose planted by the waters"); Gospel Matt 18:1–4; Offertory the Magnificat; Communion Deut 32:10–12; Postcommunion asking that "the fire of love by which St Teresa offered herself to you as a victim of charity for men" may inflame us. A decree *Urbis et Orbis* of 14 Mar 1928 extended this Mass, already granted to the Discalced Carmelites, to the universal Church.
- Source: AAS 20 pp. 148–154
- Source type: official | Rights: public-domain
- Other sources: Laveille PDF 431 quotes the same collect as the Mass prayer at the 1923 beatification. Taylor 1926/30 PDF 281 quotes a prayer "based on her words to her missionary brother": "O God, who hast inflamed with Thy love the soul of Saint Thérèse of the Child Jesus, grant that we also may love Thee and make Thee much loved."
- New to English readers: yes
- Confidence: high
### F-OM-090 — The basilica: first stone 1929, blessed 1937, consecrated 1954
- Claim: The Lisieux Sanctuary says the first stone was laid on 30 Sep 1929; the architect was Louis-Marie Cordonnier; Pius XI wanted it "very big, very beautiful, and as quickly as possible"; it was solemnly blessed on 11 Jul 1937 by Cardinal Pacelli (the future Pius XII) and consecrated on 11 Jul 1954 by Archbishop Martin of Rouen; it covers 4,500 m², is 104 m long, the dome 90 m high; mosaics and glass by Pierre Gaudin; crypt decoration finished in 1958; it suffered little from bombing in June 1944.
- Source: Sanctuary "La Basilique" page (facts-only)
- Source type: official (shrine) | Rights: facts-only
- Other sources: Pius XII's own letter of 1944 (AAS 36 p. 329) says he had been papal legate at Lisieux "in the year 1937". *C'est la confiance* n. 9 cites Pius XII's radio message for the consecration, 11 Jul 1954 (AAS 46, 404–407). The plan's "EWTN says 1936" is wrong for the blessing.
- New to English readers: partial
- Confidence: high
### F-OM-091 — Second patroness of France, 3 May 1944
- Claim: Pius XII, at the request of the Cardinal Archbishop of Paris in the name of the French cardinals, archbishops and bishops, declared her "secondary patroness of all France", with Mary as principal patroness and Joan of Arc also secondary patroness. Reasons given: in "these stormy times" and amid the spiritual and material ruins of the war, France itself was a vast mission field, and Pius XI had made her patroness of missions; "all know" the love of every class of French people for her; and the pope himself had gathered clear evidence of this as legate at Lisieux in 1937.
- Source: AAS 36 (1944) pp. 329–330
- Source type: official | Rights: facts-only
- New to English readers: partial
- Confidence: high
### F-OM-092 — First journeys of the relics, 1945–1947
- Claim: The Carmel says the relics left the Carmel for the first time after the 1944 proclamation, going to Paris in Feb–Mar 1945, then on four French tours: East (Oct 1946), West and South (Feb–Mar 1947), Centre and Alps (Apr–May 1947), North, Brittany and Normandy (Oct–Nov 1947).
- Source: Carmel "Voyages des reliques"
- Source type: official (Carmel) | Rights: unknown
- New to English readers: yes
- Confidence: high
### F-OM-093 — Paul VI and her centenary (1973)
- Claim: *C'est la confiance* says Paul VI liked to recall he was baptized on 30 Sep 1897, the day she died, and wrote a letter on her teaching to the Bishop of Bayeux and Lisieux on 2 Jan 1973 (AAS 65 (1973) 12–15).
- Source: *C'est la confiance* §6 and n. 10
- Source type: official | Rights: facts-only
- New to English readers: partial
- Confidence: high
### F-OM-094 — John Paul II at Lisieux, 2 June 1980
- Claim: On 2 Jun 1980, on his first journey to France, John Paul II visited her basilica.
- Source: *C'est la confiance* §6
- Source type: official | Rights: facts-only
- New to English readers: partial
- Confidence: high
### F-OM-095 — No primary source found for Pius X's "greatest saint of modern times"
- Claim: No primary source found. *C'est la confiance* §6 says "Saint Pius X … stated that she would become the greatest saint of modern times", but gives **no footnote** for this sentence (notes 5 and 6 cite the 1921 decree and the 1925 homily for the next sentences). The Pius X texts we did find (F-OM-011) praise the autobiography but do not say this.
- Source: *C'est la confiance* §6 (text and notes 4–13 checked in raw HTML); AAS 15 p. 205; Taylor 1924 PDF 23; Taylor 1926/30 PDF 41–42; search of all local files for "greatest saint" (only hits are Thérèse's own letters)
- Source type: official (modern) | Rights: facts-only
- New to English readers: n/a
- Confidence: high that no source is cited; the saying may come from a private remark reported later (not found).
### F-OM-096 — "Star of my pontificate": what the sources actually say
- Claim: No official Latin or Italian papal text using "star of my pontificate" was found. The nearest primary-type evidence is Pius XI's allocution of 11 Feb 1923, known to us only in English: "our beloved guiding-star, Thérèse of the Child Jesus" (Taylor). Laveille, a secondary author, writes that Pius XI "recognize[d] in her the radiant 'Star' of his pontificate" and "has proclaimed [her] Star of his pontificate, Advocate of his dearest causes, second Angel guardian of his life", and speaks of the "dear Star" of Pius XI. Taylor calls her "the star of Pius" and says the pope talked to Dougherty of "his 'guiding star'".
- Source: Taylor 1926/30 PDF 19, 25, 27, 299; Laveille PDF 427 note, 432, 439, 446
- Source type: official (English translation) + early-life | Rights: public-domain
- New to English readers: partial
- Confidence: medium — the wording "star of my pontificate" is Laveille's paraphrase; the pope's own word was "star"/"guiding star" in a translated allocution.
### F-OM-097 — World tours of the relics since 1994; reliquaries
- Claim: The Carmel says that from 1994, to prepare the 1997 centenary, the relics went to Paris, Lyon and Marseille (cities of her 1887 journey), then to capitals on five continents; Brazil kept them for a whole year ending in 1997; they have visited about 70 countries (Madagascar 2003, Bacolod in the Philippines 2000 shown). The Sanctuary lists two travelling reliquaries of Thérèse, the "Gothic" and the "Centenary" (travelling since 1997), and a reliquary of Louis and Zélie travelling since 2015; its 2026 schedule includes Metz, Amiens, Croatia and Bosnia–Herzegovina (May), Poland (Jul–Aug) and India, Tamil Nadu (Sep–Oct).
- Source: Carmel "Voyages des reliques"; Sanctuary "Voyages des reliques"
- Source type: official | Rights: unknown; facts-only
- New to English readers: partial
- Confidence: high
### F-OM-098 — Doctor of the Church, 19 October 1997: the procedure
- Claim: *Divini amoris scientia* says requests grew as the centenary of her death came near, including from bishops' conferences; the Bishop of Bayeux and Lisieux presented the formal petition on 8 Mar 1997 with the leaders of the Discalced Carmelites; the Congregation for the Doctrine of the Faith examined her "eminent doctrine" (5 May 1997) and the Congregation for the Causes of Saints the *Positio* (29 May); a joint session on 17 Jun 1997 approved unanimously; the pope announced his intention on 24 Aug 1997 at World Youth Day in Paris and proclaimed her Doctor on 19 Oct 1997, World Mission Sunday, in St Peter's. It calls her "the youngest Doctor of the Church" and "the closest to us in time" (§11), and counts her writings: three manuscripts, 266 letters, 54 poems, 8 plays, 21 prayers.
- Source: *Divini amoris scientia* §§3–4, 11–12
- Source type: official | Rights: facts-only
- New to English readers: partial (baseline has the doctorate and "third woman")
- Confidence: high
### F-OM-099 — Francis, *C'est la confiance*, 15 Oct 2023
- Claim: Francis issued the apostolic exhortation for the 150th anniversary of her birth; §6 lists the papal acts about her with AAS locators (1921 decree AAS 13 449–452; homily AAS 17 211; 1927 patronage AAS 20 147–148; 1944 patronage AAS 36 329–330; Pius XII's letter to Bishop Picaud 7 Aug 1947 and radio message 11 Jul 1954, AAS 46 404–407; Paul VI 2 Jan 1973, AAS 65 12–15; doctorate AAS 90 (1998) 409–413, 930–944) and notes that UNESCO listed her among anniversaries for 2022–2023.
- Source: *C'est la confiance* §6 and nn. 3–12
- Source type: official | Rights: facts-only
- New to English readers: partial
- Confidence: high

## Part G. Other after-death facts (people and places)
### F-OM-100 — Pierre Derrien, guardian of her tomb and sacristan
- Claim: Dolan says Pierre Derrien, a Breton, was cured of a grave illness in 1904 after praying to her, went to Lisieux to give thanks, later went alone to Rome and was received by Pius X ("You have the faith, my son, and the good God will bless you"), and was then offered by Mère Agnès the post of guardian of her tomb; in 1923 he prepared the 160 Masses said in the chapel on beatification day, and Cardinal Vico said of him « Voilà un sacristain qui a de la tête ».
- Source: Dolan PDF 136–138
- Source type: popular (interview) | Rights: public-domain
- New to English readers: yes (in a forgotten book)
- Confidence: medium
### F-OM-101 — The Martin sisters at the time of the canonization
- Claim: Taylor 1924 says all the Martin sisters except Thérèse were alive in 1924; the Guérin cousin Jeanne (Mme La Néele) alone among relatives outside the cloister accompanied the 1923 translation and saw the beatification; Isidore Guérin died in 1909. Taylor 1926/30 says in March 1927 Mme La Néele was "the sole surviving relative of the Saint outside of the cloister"; that Mère Marie de Gonzague was alive when he first came in 1903 (she died 17 Dec 1904, Laveille PDF 402 note).
- Source: Taylor 1924 PDF 24, 30; Taylor 1926/30 PDF 38; Laveille PDF 402 note
- Source type: old-collection | Rights: public-domain
- New to English readers: partial
- Confidence: high
### F-OM-102 — The birthplace in Alençon kept by the convert Grants
- Claim: Rev. Alexander Grant, former United Free Church minister of Lochranza (Arran), received into the Church at Edinburgh on 20 Apr 1911, moved with his wife to 42 rue Saint-Blaise, Alençon, on 21 May 1912 to care for the house where she was born; he died there on 19 Jul 1917; Mrs Grant still kept it in 1927; a small chapel was being built for pilgrims; a tablet on the balcony marked the birthplace, and the baptismal entry was shown at Notre-Dame.
- Source: Taylor 1926/30 PDF 36–37 notes, 422–423; Taylor 1924 PDF 28 note, 432; Laveille PDF 416–417
- Source type: old-collection | Rights: public-domain
- New to English readers: yes
- Confidence: high
### F-OM-103 — Objects kept in the Carmel sacristy by the 1920s
- Claim: Taylor, via Cardinal Dougherty's story, lists "the more precious souvenirs of the Saint" kept behind a wrought-iron screen in the Carmel sacristy: the long tresses of her hair, her habit and First Communion dress, her instruments of penance, the palm found in the coffin in 1910, and other things.
- Source: Taylor 1926/30 PDF 463
- Source type: popular | Rights: public-domain
- New to English readers: yes
- Confidence: medium
### F-OM-104 — The Carfin grotto in Scotland as a Little Flower shrine
- Claim: Taylor, parish priest of Carfin, says the grotto begun by villagers in Sep 1920 opened on Rosary Sunday 1922; a shrine of Thérèse was added; after her statue was set up in May 1923 about a quarter of a million pilgrims came in less than three months; a marble statue was erected on Pentecost 1925; 30,000 came on her first feast (Oct 1925). When police in June 1924 invoked an old penal law to stop a Corpus Christi procession, the protest led to the Roman Catholic Relief Act passed in December 1926 (per *The Universe* and *Columba*, quoted by Taylor).
- Source: Taylor 1926/30 PDF 28–29, 432–436; Taylor 1924 PDF 29 note
- Source type: eyewitness (interested party) | Rights: public-domain
- New to English readers: yes
- Confidence: medium — Taylor is an interested witness; the Relief Act link is his and the press's.
### F-OM-106 — Père Perroy defended her against a "rose-garden" picture of her
- Claim: Laveille says that at Lyon cathedral in 1923 Père Perroy, fearing that all the roses would give people a false idea of her holiness, said: "let me defend you against those who represent you as walking at your ease in a fragrant rose garden … your soul was above all virile … you could take your place between Joan of Arc and Margaret Mary … you have chosen the way most contrary to nature, the way of little ones."
- Source: Laveille PDF 433, citing the 1923 album p. 140
- Source type: early-life (quoted sermon) | Rights: public-domain
- New to English readers: yes
- Confidence: high for the quotation
### F-OM-108 — Fr Dubosq: once Charles Anne's seminary superior, later Promoter of the Faith
- Claim: Taylor notes that the superior of the Bayeux seminary when Charles Anne was cured (1906) was Fr Dubosq, SS, formerly professor at the Sulpician seminary in Paris, who later became Promoter of the Faith in her canonization process.
- Source: Taylor 1926/30 PDF 276
- Source type: old-collection | Rights: public-domain
- New to English readers: yes
- Confidence: high

## Part G. Additions from the Carmel archive (second pass), connections and surprises

Base URL for the cards below: https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/naissance-dune-sainte/ ("Carmel"). Pages were read through a fetch tool that summarizes; a few summary lines looked wrong and are flagged. Rights for all Carmel pages: unknown (facts only).
### F-OM-109 — The apostolic process: 58 sessions to 25 Aug 1916 and 91 sessions to 30 Oct 1917 are two phases, not a conflict
- Claim: The Carmel's process page says the Apostolic Process ran 58 sessions in less than a year (1915–1916), heard about 20 witnesses in wartime Normandy, used 66 standard questions, and concluded on 25 Aug 1916. The tomb page numbers later sessions 80, 81, 82 (9, 10, 11 Aug 1917) and the final session 91 at Bayeux cathedral on 30 Oct 1917. So the 1915–16 phase (58 sessions) was followed by the 1916–17 phase.
- When / where: 17 Mar 1915 – 30 Oct 1917, Bayeux
- Source: Carmel /les-proces-la-sainte-de-therese/le-proces-apostolique/ ; /le-proces-apostolique/les-temoignages-du-proces-apostolique/ ; /le-proces-apostolique/les-exhumations-de-therese/au-proces-apostolique-verification-de-la-tombe-et-reconnaissance-des-restes-de-la-servante-de-dieu/ ; see F-OM-036
- Source type: official (Carmel) | Rights: unknown
- Other sources: Taylor 1926/30 PDF 274–275 (91st and last session, 30 Oct 1917).
- New to English readers: yes
- Confidence: medium — the link between the two counts is our reading; no page states "phase one ended at session 58".
- Notes: This settles conflict 16 in the gaps file as a difference of phase (the plan's "58 sessions, 25 Aug 1916" is the first phase). The Carmel's own "inchoative / continuative" wording (F-OM-036) has the second phase opening on 1 Apr 1916, which overlaps the 25 Aug 1916 date; treat the overlap as unresolved.
### F-OM-111 — Ex-votos: 50 in 1911, 460 by 1917
- Claim: The Carmel's process page says the ex-voto count at the Carmel rose from 50 in 1911 to 460 in 1917.
- Source: Carmel /le-proces-apostolique/
- Source type: official (Carmel) | Rights: unknown
- New to English readers: yes
- Confidence: medium — one figure, no breakdown.
### F-OM-113 — First exhumation, 6 Sep 1910, in the Carmel's account: 3.5 m deep, violet perfume, sealed parchment
- Claim: Present were Bishop Lemonnier, Mgr de Teil, the vicars general Canons Quirié and Dubosq, tribunal priests, workmen and several hundred people. The coffin lay 3.5 m down, in poor condition. The veils had gone and the wool habit crumbled; only bones remained, as she had wished. Through the loose planks the palm showed "toute verte et fraîche comme au premier jour". Two workmen said they smelled "a sweet, strong violet perfume". The bishop forbade relic-taking. The bones were dressed anew, the head veiled and ringed with roses from the Carmel garden, and shown about 45 minutes to over 500 people who touched objects to them. A parchment was sealed in the lead coffin, which went into a new brick vault.
- When / where: 6 Sep 1910, Lisieux cemetery
- Source: Carmel /les-exhumations-de-therese/a-lisieux-les-recits-anciens-des-deux-exhumations/ (extract from *Pluie de roses* 1911, pp. 103–109)
- Source type: official (Carmel), from a 1911 printed account | Rights: unknown
- Other sources: F-OM-028 (Laveille, Taylor: "sweet perfume").
- New to English readers: yes (violet; 3.5 m; the vault)
- Confidence: medium — "violet" is the workmen's report; Laveille and Taylor speak only of "sweet perfume".
### F-OM-114 — Second exhumation, 9–10 Aug 1917: the ribbon with her saying, the new coffin, and the guard of 3,000
- Claim: On 9 Aug the oak and lead coffin was moved to the cemetery chapel. Bishop Lemonnier intoned *Laudate pueri Dominum* and pronounced excommunication on anyone who took fragments. A soldier touched his helmet to the coffin and kissed it. About 3,000 people kept vigil, with two soldiers on leave among the guard. Work began at 3.15 am on 10 Aug. A white silk ribbon with gold letters, « Je veux passer mon ciel à faire du bien sur la terre. Après ma mort, je ferai tomber une pluie de roses », was found well kept. It had come from a bouquet on her tomb in Aug 1910 and tied the roses put in the coffin on 6 Sep 1910. The bones were wrapped by the two Carmelites and priests in linen and silk and put in a carved oak casket (1.2 × 0.4 × 0.3 m) lined with white satin, its lid showing the Holy Face and instruments of the Passion. The casket went into a lead coffin and a rosewood sarcophagus with silver handles and fourteen silver bolts, a gift of the firm Borniol. The Latin plate read « HIC OSSA ANCILLAE DEI THERESIAE A PUERO JESU DEPOSITA SUNT DIE DECIMA AUGUSTI MCMXVII ». Workmen asked to carry it to the vault. Seals were placed on all remaining hair and body fragments until the Holy See decided on reliquaries.
- Source: Carmel /les-exhumations-de-therese/a-lisieux-les-recits-anciens-des-deux-exhumations/ (from *Souvenir de la deuxième exhumation…*, approved for printing 15 Aug 1917 by Bishop Thomas)
- Source type: official (Carmel), from a 1917 printed account | Rights: unknown
- Other sources: F-OM-039 (Laveille, Taylor: carved oak chest in rosewood coffin lined with lead).
- New to English readers: yes
- Confidence: medium — from a summary; the ribbon wording matches the cross saying in Laveille (quotation 1).
- Notes: The Carmel page also reports a station gatekeeper saying "nothing will ever be too beautiful for Sister Thérèse" (as the summary renders it).
### F-OM-115 — The 1917 tomb inquiry: sessions 80–82, the enclosure, the cross, the witnesses
- Claim: Session 80 (9 Aug 1917) heard three witnesses on the grave: Abbé Alexandre-Charles Maupas (b. 27 Aug 1850), parish priest of Saint-Jacques and Carmel superior, present at the 1897 burial and 1910 exhumation; Sr Marie-Élisabeth Hamard (1860–1935), extern sister; Jeanne Bonnelle (b. 26 Jul 1877), cemetery keeper since 1912. The Carmelite plot was about 10 m by 5.5 m, walled and railed. The 1897 grave was in the north-east corner; the 1910 grave near the entrance on the left. The iron cross read "1873–1897. Sister Thérèse of the Child Jesus and the Holy Face. 'I want to spend my heaven doing good on earth.'" Session 81 (10 Aug) opened the coffin; session 82 (11 Aug) heard the doctors.
- Source: Carmel /au-proces-apostolique-verification-de-la-tombe-et-reconnaissance-des-restes-de-la-servante-de-dieu/
- Source type: official (Carmel) | Rights: unknown
- New to English readers: yes
- Confidence: medium (summary).
### F-OM-116 — The doctors' report on her bones, 11 Aug 1917
- Claim: Dr Alexandre-Damase de Cornière (b. 26 Oct 1841, retired head surgeon of Lisieux hospital) found a complete skull with sutures intact, ten upper teeth, a complete spine with 23 ribs, a complete pelvis and long bones. Dr Paul Loisnel (b. 23 May 1862, hospital surgeon and medical examiner) judged the skeleton female, aged about 20–25, about 1.59–1.60 m tall, with pelvic measures of 12.8, 13.5 and 14.5 cm.
- Source: Carmel /au-proces-apostolique-verification-de-la-tombe…/
- Source type: official (Carmel) | Rights: unknown
- Other sources: Loisnel and de Cornière are also witnesses in the miracle files (M-OM-001, M-OM-002).
- New to English readers: yes
- Confidence: medium (summary; figures should be checked on the page).
- Notes: De Cornière was the Carmel doctor who treated her in 1897 (M-OM-001 note). Surprise: her height as measured from her bones.
### F-OM-120 — The French text of the canonization bull on the Carmel site, and the feast date
- Claim: The Carmel's French bull names the four cures as Sœur Louise de Saint-Germain (haemorrhagic gastric ulcer), Charles Anne (pulmonary tuberculosis with haemoptysis, cavity stage), Gabrielle Trimusi (knee and spine; a novena began 13 Jun 1923) and Maria Pellemans (lungs and intestines, March 1923). It sets her feast on 3 October and asks for "a rain of roses".
- Source: Carmel /la-beatification-et-la-canonisation/bulle-de-la-canonisation/
- Source type: official (Carmel translation) | Rights: unknown
- Other sources: AAS 17 pp. 337–347 (Latin); F-OM-079.
- New to English readers: partial
- Confidence: medium — the summary agrees with the Latin.
### F-OM-121 — Conflict: the Carmel's French brief says the miracles decree was "13 Feb 1923"
- Claim: The Carmel's French text of *Quod Ioannes*, as summarized by the fetch tool, gives 13 Feb 1923 for the decree on the two miracles. The Latin decree is dated 11 Feb 1923 (AAS 15 pp. 168–170; F-OM-051). The summary also says the brief describes the illness as caused "by the demon" (agreeing with F-OM-004), and that the parents were "Louis-Joseph-Stanislas" (the Latin bull has "Aloisio Stanislao").
- Source: Carmel /la-beatification-et-la-canonisation/bref-de-la-beatification/
- Source type: official (Carmel translation) | Rights: unknown
- New to English readers: partial
- Confidence: low for the 13 Feb date — probably a slip in the summary or the translation; the Latin controls.
- Notes: Do not use 13 Feb.
### F-OM-122 — Two 1915 charcoal drawings of Thérèse "at the front", with halos added later
- Claim: The Carmel says two charcoal drawings ordered in 1915 from Charles Jouvenot and Pierre Léon Adolphe Annould show her at the front. A halo was added to one after the beatification and to the other after the canonization (the second probably by Céline). Seven numbered images and seven postcards sent to the Carmel in the war were probably shown to Annould. Jouvenot also drew soldiers' accounts, kept as colour glass plates.
- Source: Carmel /therese-et-la-premiere-guerre-mondiale/images-et-cartes-postales/ ; /selection-de-courrier-illustre/
- Source type: official (Carmel) | Rights: unknown
- New to English readers: yes
- Confidence: medium.
- Notes: Surprise: the halos were added afterwards, so the image tracked her Church status.
### F-OM-125 — Benedict XVI, 2011: the sources on her parents, her doctorate, and her missionary brothers
- Claim: In the audience of 6 Apr 2011 (as summarized) he says her parents Louis and Zélie Martin were beatified together on 19 Oct 2008; that the Martin family had nine children, five of whom (daughters) became religious (the summary's wording; check it); and he names Paul, Augustine, John of the Cross, Thomas Aquinas, Francis and Dominic. John Paul II's 1997 homily (as summarized) says she supported the missionaries Fr Maurice Bellière and Fr Adolphe Roulland by letters and prayer, and calls her the youngest Doctor.
- Source: https://www.vatican.va/content/benedict-xvi/en/audiences/2011/documents/hf_ben-xvi_aud_20110406.html ; https://www.vatican.va/content/john-paul-ii/en/homilies/1997/documents/hf_jp-ii_hom_19101997.html
- Source type: official | Rights: facts-only
- New to English readers: no
- Confidence: low–medium — the fetch summary of the 2011 audience also gave "entered Carmel November 1888" and "patroness of missions 1939", which are wrong (April 1888; 14 Dec 1927, F-OM-087). We did not use those. "Nine siblings" was garbled; check the text before use.

### Connections to other saints and causes (cards)
### F-OM-126 — Connection: Joan of Arc (patroness of France, and a cause "saved")
- Claim: In 1944 Pius XII made her secondary patroness of France alongside Joan of Arc (F-OM-091). Cardinal Touchet credited her with saving Joan's cause from interruption under Pius X (F-OM-062). Père Perroy set her "between Joan of Arc and Margaret Mary" (F-OM-106).
- Source: AAS 36 pp. 329–330; Laveille PDF 433, 436
- Source type: official; early-life | Rights: facts-only; public-domain
- New to English readers: partial
- Confidence: high for the 1944 act; low–medium for the Touchet claim.
- Notes: Connection card. Joan was canonized in 1920, five years before her.
### F-OM-127 — Connection: Francis Xavier (equal patron of missions) and Théophane Vénard
- Claim: The 1927 decree makes her patroness of all missions "equal in rank" with Francis Xavier (F-OM-087). Taylor says Maryknoll pushed vocations in 1926–27 "under the united patronage of the Little Flower and Blessed Theophane Venard", and that Fr Roulland met Taylor in the rue du Bac seminary where Vénard's relics lie.
- Source: AAS 20 pp. 147–148; Taylor 1926/30 PDF 452 and note
- Source type: official; old-collection | Rights: public-domain
- New to English readers: yes (the Maryknoll pairing)
- Confidence: medium.
- Notes: Connection card. Vénard was blessed in 1909 (canonized 1988, outside these sources).
### F-OM-128 — Connection: Teresa of Avila and John of the Cross
- Claim: Taylor says she was the first daughter of St Teresa canonized "after three centuries and a half" (F-OM-075). Taylor prints her motto, taken from St John of the Cross: "Love is repaid by love alone" (Taylor 1926/30 PDF 318). Benedict XVI names John of the Cross among the saints he cites (F-OM-125).
- Source: Taylor 1926/30 PDF 20, 318
- Source type: old-collection | Rights: public-domain
- New to English readers: partial
- Confidence: medium.
- Notes: Connection card. See F-OM-075 on the Mary Magdalene de' Pazzi qualification.
### F-OM-129 — Connection: Raphael Kalinowski, the Polish Carmelite (canonized in 1991)
- Claim: Fr Raphael of St Joseph (Kalinowski), Vicar Provincial at Wadowice, wrote in 1902 that her poem "To live by Love" calmed him in a night of trouble, after he had once said Polish could not carry her style (M-OM-051; W-OM-006). Taylor's note says he "died in the odour of sanctity in 1907", cause submitted.
- Source: Taylor 1924 PDF 393–394
- Source type: old-collection | Rights: public-domain
- New to English readers: yes
- Confidence: medium.
- Notes: Connection card. His canonization in 1991 is outside these sources; check before the writer states it.
### F-OM-130 — Connection: Almire Pichon SJ and the Martin parents
- Claim: Pichon, the Jesuit who directed her, testified in the apostolic process on 23 Apr 1915 (F-OM-110). Her parents were beatified together on 19 Oct 2008 (Benedict XVI, F-OM-125).
- Source: Carmel witness page; Benedict XVI audience
- Source type: official | Rights: unknown; facts-only
- Confidence: medium.
- Notes: Connection card. Later canonization of the parents is outside these sources.
### F-OM-131 — Connection: Cardinal Vico, papal legate, and the "guiding star" of two popes
- Claim: Cardinal Vico, prefect of the Congregation of Rites, signed the 1921 and 1927 decrees, received Pius XI's "rain of roses" letter (14 May 1923), and was papal legate at Lisieux on 30 Sep 1925 (F-OM-060, 081, 087). Pius XI called her his "guiding star" (F-OM-052, F-OM-096).
- Source: AAS 15 pp. 283–284; AAS 20 p. 147; Laveille PDF 444
- Source type: official | Rights: public-domain
- Confidence: high for the acts.
- Notes: Connection card to other Church figures; Pius XI's own canonization is outside this family.

### Surprises (cards)
### F-OM-132 — Surprise: the Church's own brief says her family thought the illness was from the devil
- Claim: See F-OM-004 and F-OM-005: the brief of 1923 says the family believed the childhood illness came from the devil, and the 1921 decree treated it as an objection.
- New to English readers: yes
- Confidence: high.
- Notes: Marked "surprise". The writer must attribute the belief to the family, as the brief does.
### F-OM-133 — Surprise: her bones tell us her height
- Claim: In 1917 Dr Loisnel judged her height at about 1.59–1.60 m and her age 20–25 from the skeleton (F-OM-116).
- Confidence: medium.
- Notes: Marked "surprise". Compare with the height given in other dossier parts.
### F-OM-135 — Surprise: soldiers, not bishops, drove the dispensation; the halos on the war drawings came later
- Claim: See F-OM-037 (26 soldiers' petitions, 1916–18; the Carmel says they were decisive for the dispensation from the reputation inquiry) and F-OM-122 (halos added after beatification and canonization).
- Confidence: medium.
- Notes: Marked "surprise".
